After castling, the king and rook end up on the same squares as in standard chess (king on g-file and rook on f-file for kingside, king on c-file and rook on d-file for queenside). Castling is written in UCI as the king moving onto its own rook's square (e.g., 'e1h1' or 'b1a1'), exactly as it appears in 'get_legal_moves'.
Now is your turn to make a move. Before making a move you can pick one of the following actions:
- 'get_current_board' to get the schema and current status of the board
- 'get_legal_moves' to get a UCI formatted list of available moves
- 'make_move <UCI formatted move>' when you are ready to complete your turn (e.g., 'make_move e2e4')
Reply with plain text containing only the action, e.g. 'make_move e2e4'. Do not use XML, tags or tool-call syntax.

Player_Black (to Proxy):

The position is a forced mate. Rook f3 to h3 gives check, and the king has no escape:

- Kxh3 is impossible because the rook is protected by the queen on g4 and the knight on g1.
- g2 and g3 are covered by the queen on g4.
- h1 is covered by the rook through the king's square.
- Kxg1 is illegal because the queen on g4 defends g1 along the g-file.

Castling is written in UCI as the king moving onto its own rook's square (e.g., 'e1h1' or 'b1a1'), exactly as it appears in 'get_legal_moves'.
